**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial reasoning rule is: "If XXX is at location YYY at time ZZZ then XXX cannot be at a different location at time ZZZ"

**Step 3:** Apply the spatial reasoning rule

According to this rule, Mia cannot be running both to the left (White's testimony) and to the right (May's testimony) at the same time after dodging an attack. These are mutually exclusive directions of movement at the same moment in time.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is clear - White claims Mia ran left after dodging, while May claims Mia ran right after dodging. Both cannot be true simultaneously as Mia cannot run in two opposite directions at once.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Phoenix's testimony (Testimony 3): "After I shoved him, he... He fell down on top of his cheap umbrella."

Looking at Crime Photo 1 (Evidence 2): The caption states "The victim is lying head down... An umbrella with a bent handle lies open in a distance."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The key spatial relationship here involves the victim's position relative to the umbrella. According to Phoenix's testimony, the victim fell ON TOP of the umbrella. However, the crime photo shows the umbrella "lies open in a distance" - implying the umbrella is separate from the victim's body.

**Step 3:** Apply relevant spatial reasoning rules

The rule "If XXX falls onto YYY and YYY is covered by ZZZ then XXX is covered by ZZZ" is relevant here. If the victim (XXX) fell onto the umbrella (YYY), then the victim should be covering or on top of the umbrella. However, the crime photo shows the umbrella is "in a distance" - meaning the victim is not covering the umbrella, and they are spatially separated.

**Step 4:** Explain the contradiction

Phoenix claims the victim fell directly on top of the umbrella, which would place the victim's body in contact with or covering the umbrella. However, the crime scene photo clearly shows the umbrella is positioned at a distance from the victim's body, contradicting Phoenix's account of the spatial relationship between the victim and the umbrella.

While no specific spatial rule directly applies, we can reason that:
- A camera set up for meteor showers would typically face upward toward the sky
- A camera facing the lake (horizontal direction) would not be optimally positioned for photographing meteor showers in the sky above
- These two orientations are spatially incompatible for the stated purpose

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial contradiction lies in the camera's orientation. Testimony 3 claims the camera was set up for meteor showers (requiring upward/angled skyward orientation), while Evidence 2 states the camera faces the lake (horizontal orientation). These are mutually exclusive spatial orientations for the stated purpose of photographing meteor showers.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The rule "If XXX is at location YYY at time ZZZ then XXX cannot be at a different location at time ZZZ" applies here. ------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

Lang's Testimony 3 states that the president's body was hidden "inside that monster costume over there." However, Evidence 5 (Crime Scene Notes) indicates that the body was found "lying face-up on top of the dirt," with "no dirt on the front of the body." This suggests the body was placed on top of the dirt, not inside any costume or container that would have dirt inside it.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction.

The spatial reasoning rules provided primarily deal with relative positioning (inside, in front of, behind, etc.) and visibility blocking. None of these rules directly address the contradiction between a body being "inside" something versus "on top of" something else.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction.

No applicable rules exist from the provided list.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction.

The contradiction lies in the spatial relationship of the body to its surroundings. Testimony 3 claims the body was hidden *inside* a monster costume, which would imply the body was enclosed within the costume. However, Evidence 5 shows the body was found *on top of* the dirt, with no dirt on its front, indicating it was placed directly on the ground rather than inside any container or costume. This is a direct spatial contradiction - the body cannot simultaneously be inside a costume and lying exposed on top of the dirt.

No applicable spatial reasoning rules from the provided list directly address this contradiction.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction lies in the spatial location of the actual crime. Franziska claims the crime scene is the P.I.C. meeting room based on bloodstains there, but the autopsy shows the chest wounds (from the candelabra) were inflicted post-mortem, meaning the victim was already dead when those wounds were made. However, examining the evidence, specifically Evidence 8 (Kay's Recollections): "Kay remembers a person coming from beyond the concession stand and pushing her off of the building." This indicates Kay was pushed from the concession stand area, which is on the right-most edge of the viewing platform according to Evidence 7 (Bigg Building Pamphlet).

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** Since no applicable rules exist, proceed to Step 4

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction lies in the spatial positioning. According to the Bigg Building Pamphlet (Evidence 7), the cherry tree is on the left-most edge of the viewing platform, while the candy/concession stand is on the right-most edge. These are opposite ends of the platform. 

Kay's testimony (Testimony 3) places her under the cherry tree (left side), but her recollection (Evidence 8) states she was pushed from beyond the concession stand (right side). This creates a spatial impossibility - she cannot simultaneously be under the cherry tree on the left and be pushed from the concession stand on the right. The distance between these two locations makes it impossible for both statements to be true simultaneously.

This indicates there is a ladder in the storeroom that goes upwards, which would be another exit from the storeroom besides the hatch to the viewing platform.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rules provided are primarily about containment, covering, and relative positioning (inside, covered by, in front of, etc.). The contradiction here is about the existence of exits from a space (storeroom), which is not directly addressed by the given spatial reasoning rules.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

None of the listed spatial reasoning rules directly apply to this contradiction about the number of exits from a space.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning contradiction is straightforward: Testimony 3 claims the storeroom has only one exit (the hatch to the viewing platform), while Evidence 17 shows a ladder going upwards from the storeroom, indicating at least one additional exit. **Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction lies in Acro's claim that he couldn't look out the window to see the victim's position, yet the Crime Photo shows the victim was clearly visible from above (the window position). - If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY
- If XXX is in a different room from YYY then XXX cannot see YYY

However, Atmey claims the thief grabbed a weapon "from the side" - which would be impossible if Atmey's back was against the wall, as there would be no "side" for the thief to approach from.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial reasoning rules are:
- "If XXX has their back to YYY then XXX cannot see YYY"
- "If XXX is facing YYY then XXX can see YYY"

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The contradiction is revealed through spatial reasoning about positioning:
- If Atmey had his back to the wall (as stated in Testimony 5), then the only direction the thief could be is in front of him
- However, Testimony 2 claims the weapon came "from the side" - which would be impossible if Atmey's back was against the wall
- This creates an impossible spatial configuration where both statements cannot be true simultaneously

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning shows that Atmey's two testimonies describe mutually exclusive physical positions. You cannot simultaneously have your back against a wall AND have someone approach you from the side to strike you with a weapon.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the crime scene photo (Evidence 9), we see the victim sitting on a chair with a knife in his chest. There's a glass of juice behind him on the dresser, and a full bottle of juice on the desk in front of him. The guitar case is on the floor next to him, and there's a smashed vase that has stained the carpet with water.

In Andrews' testimony (Testimony 5), she states: "I was the one who knocked the flower vase over, where it fell onto the guitar case."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The key spatial relationship here involves the water from the smashed vase. According to the evidence, the guitar case has water "only on top of the lid" (Evidence 7). However, if the vase fell onto the guitar case, we would expect water to be on the case itself, not just on top of the lid.

**Step 3:** Apply spatial reasoning rules

Using the rule: "If XXX is touches YYY then YYY is touches XXX" - if the vase fell onto the guitar case, then the guitar case would be touching the vase and its contents (water). However, the evidence states water is only on top of the lid, which suggests the vase didn't actually fall onto the case but rather the water splashed onto the lid from a different position.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning contradiction

The spatial arrangement described in the testimony (vase falling directly onto the guitar case) contradicts the physical evidence (water only on top of the lid). If the vase had fallen directly onto the guitar case as claimed, water would be distributed across the case itself, not just on the lid. This suggests the vase was knocked over from a different position, causing water to splash onto the lid rather than the case being directly under the falling vase.

------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the testimonies, Ini repeatedly states that her sister Mimi was driving the car during the accident (Testimony 5: "I ended up in the passenger's seat that night too"). However, Evidence 6 (Newspaper Clipping 2) contains Ini's interview where she states: "I was like, really tired and I dropped off to sleep... I opened the right door and like, got out."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rules focus on visibility, location, and physical positioning. However, this contradiction involves the spatial positioning of who was in the driver's seat versus the passenger's seat.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

While not explicitly listed, we can apply spatial reasoning: If Ini was in the passenger's seat (right side), then she could not have been the driver. However, her statement about opening the "right door" to escape suggests she was on the right side of the vehicle, which would be the passenger's seat in countries where people drive on the right side of the road (like the US). This aligns with her testimony.

But there's a deeper contradiction: Ini's testimonies consistently claim Mimi was driving and wouldn't let Ini drive (Testimonies 3-5), yet the newspaper clipping shows Ini giving an interview where she implies she was the one who "dropped off to sleep" while driving, which contradicts her claim that Mimi was driving.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial contradiction lies in the physical positioning of who was in the driver's seat. Ini's testimonies claim Mimi was driving (driver's seat) and Ini was in the passenger's seat. However, the newspaper clipping contains Ini's statement that suggests she was the one driving when she fell asleep, which directly contradicts her testimonies about being in the passenger's seat.

- Picking up the coffee cup with his "free hand" (Testimony 4)

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial reasoning rules are:
- If right-hand fingerprints of HHH are on XXX then HHH held XXX with the right hand
- If left-hand fingerprints of HHH are on XXX then HHH held XXX with the left hand

**Step 3:** Apply the rules to reveal the contradiction

Given that the Coffee Cup has the victim's fingerprints (Evidence 13), and the handle is on the right side when facing the cup, this strongly suggests the victim would have used his right hand to hold the cup (as most people would naturally reach for a right-positioned handle with their right hand).

This directly contradicts Victor Kudo's testimony that the victim picked up the cup with his "free hand" (left hand) while holding the newspaper in his right hand. The spatial positioning of the cup's handle makes it highly unlikely someone would use their left hand to pick it up while their right hand was occupied.

**Step 4:** The spatial reasoning shows that the cup's handle position (right side) contradicts the testimony about using the "free" (left) hand to pick it up while the right hand held a newspaper.

However, the Witness's Photo (Evidence 6) shows the victim facing left (west) and the man in jail uniform facing her from the left (west side). This means the photo captures the west side of the bridge from the south, which should be impossible due to the obstructing rocks. The spatial rule "If XXX blocks the view of YYY then YYY cannot be seen" directly applies - the rocks (XXX) block the view of the west side (YYY), so the west side cannot be seen from the south where Melissa claims to have taken the photo.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is confirmed by the spatial reasoning rule

The rocks blocking the view from south to west makes it impossible for Melissa to have taken the photo showing the west side of the bridge from her claimed position in the south.

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the evidence:
- Evidence 6 (Witness's Photo) shows Valerie wearing a scarf and standing to the right, facing left, close to the broken end of the bridge
- Evidence 4 (Crime Photo) shows Valerie dead in the trunk with no scarf, wearing a coat that is dry and clean
- Evidence 5 (Scarf) was found on Dusky Bridge, wet and muddy

Looking at the testimonies:
- Testimony 3 states the criminal shoved the victim down from behind and stabbed her in the back
- Testimony 4 claims "...That must have been when the scarf fell off"

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The key spatial issue is: if Valerie was stabbed from behind (as per Testimony 3), she would fall forward (toward the broken end of the bridge). However, the scarf was found on the bridge (wet and muddy), while Valerie ended up in the car trunk without the scarf.

**Step 3:** Apply relevant spatial reasoning rules

Using the rule: "If XXX is hit from behind then XXX falls forward"
- Valerie was hit from behind (stabbed in the back)
- Therefore, Valerie would fall forward (toward the broken end of the bridge)
- This would cause her to fall OFF the bridge, not remain on it where the scarf was found

**Step 4:** Explain the contradiction

The contradiction lies between Testimony 3 (criminal shoved victim from behind and stabbed her in the back) and the physical evidence. If Valerie was stabbed from behind while standing close to the broken end, she would have fallen forward off the bridge. However, the scarf was found on the bridge (wet and muddy), suggesting it came off while she was still on the bridge - which contradicts the forward fall that would have taken her off the bridge entirely.

**Step 5:** Provide answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 6, "testimony": 3}

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at the spatial arrangement described in the evidence:
- Evidence 2 (Dusky Bridge Map) shows the bridge is broken midway towards the east
- Evidence 6 (Witness's Photo) shows the victim standing close to the broken end (east side), facing left (west)
- The man (Fawles) is standing to the left (west), facing the victim

In Fawles's Testimony 3, he states: "Finally, one woman came. She stood front of me."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The key spatial rule here is: "If XXX has their back to YYY then XXX cannot see YYY" and "If XXX is facing YYY then XXX can see YYY"

**Step 3:** Apply the spatial reasoning rules

According to the Witness's Photo (Evidence 6), the victim is facing left (west) while standing on the east side of the bridge. Fawles is standing to the left (west) of her, facing her. This means the victim is facing Fawles directly - she is facing west, and Fawles is to her west.

However, if the victim "stood front of me" as Fawles claims in Testimony 3, this would imply she approached from the opposite direction (from the west) and faced east toward Fawles. But the photo shows her facing west, which contradicts this spatial arrangement.

**Step 4:** The contradiction revealed

The spatial contradiction is that the Witness's Photo shows the victim facing west (toward Fawles who is on the west side), but if she "stood front of me" as Fawles claims, she would have been facing east. The photo's spatial arrangement shows the victim facing away from the direction she would have come from if she approached Fawles from the west side of the bridge.

{"evidence": 6, "testimony": 3}

This means Melissa could not have seen the killer carry the body to the car and put it in the trunk, as the parking lot would be out of her line of sight.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning

Melissa's testimony claims she saw the killer carry the body to the car and hide it in the trunk. However, given her position south of the bridge and the obstruction by huge rocks, she could not have seen the parking lot area where the car was located. This creates a direct contradiction between what she claims to have witnessed and what was physically possible from her vantage point.

- Victim gained 5,700 points (9,200 - 3,500)

The chip photo (Evidence 6) shows a spatial distribution that contradicts the implied flow of the game described in the testimonies. The victim's massive chip lead (9,200 vs starting 3,500) cannot be explained by the defendant simply losing their remaining 1,400 chips in one final hand, as this would only account for 1,400 of the victim's 5,700 point gain.

However, Evidence 18 (the Lighter) shows it has both Athena's prints AND Clay Terran's fingerprints and blood on it. This creates a spatial contradiction - if the lighter was held by the killer (as Fulbright claims), then it should only have the killer's prints, but it also has the victim's prints and blood.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rules provided focus on physical relationships (locked doors, bullet trajectories, falling directions, containment, distance, etc.). None of these rules directly address fingerprint evidence or the implications of multiple sets of prints on an object.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

No applicable spatial reasoning rules from the provided list directly address this fingerprint contradiction.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction lies in the physical evidence itself. For the lighter to be "proof" that Athena is the killer (as Fulbright claims in Testimony 3), it would need to show exclusive contact by Athena. However, Evidence 18 shows the lighter has both Athena's prints AND the victim's (Clay Terran's) fingerprints and blood. This spatial overlap of prints from both suspect and victim on the same object contradicts Fulbright's claim that this lighter alone proves Athena's guilt, as it suggests the victim also handled the lighter.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The rule "If XXX is inside YYY then YYY is contains XXX" applies here. The elevator (XXX) is inside the lounge (YYY), therefore the lounge contains the elevator.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- The elevator is inside the lounge
- Therefore, the lounge contains the elevator
- Since the victim's body was inside the elevator (as stated in Evidence 2), the victim was also inside the lounge
- This directly contradicts Testimony 3's claim that Edgeworth was "the only person in this lounge the entire time"

Looking at the Study Photo (Evidence 3): Shows "the victim is lying in the study next to floor-to-ceiling windows with clear glass. He is covered by books."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The key spatial relationship here involves the victim's position relative to the bookshelves and windows.

**Step 3:** Apply spatial reasoning rules

From the Study Diagram (Evidence 4), we know: "There is a tree outside the large floor-to-ceiling window. On the other side of the window is the bookshelf."

This establishes that the bookshelves are on the opposite side of the room from the windows.

Atishon claims he saw the murder "from outside his study window" (Testimony 1), and that the doctor was "standing in front of the bookshelves" (Testimony 3). However, the Study Photo (Evidence 3) shows the victim lying next to the windows, not in front of the bookshelves. 

Since the bookshelves are on the opposite side of the room from the windows (as shown in the Study Diagram), Atishon could not have seen the doctor standing in front of the bookshelves while viewing from outside the window. The spatial arrangement makes this impossible - the bookshelves would be on the far side of the room from the window, and the victim is shown lying near the windows.

**Step 4:** The contradiction is clear through basic spatial reasoning - the victim's position in the photo (near the windows) contradicts Atishon's claim that the doctor was standing in front of the bookshelves when viewed from outside the window.

However, Evidence 5 (Notice) states that "All of the hallway shutters will remain shut for today's Dance of Devotion." This creates a spatial contradiction - if the shutters were shut, Andistan'dhin could not have seen through the windows to observe Ahlbi in the treasure room.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial reasoning rule is: "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY" - but this is about being in the same room, not about visibility through windows.

However, we can apply a more fundamental spatial principle: if shutters are closed (Evidence 5), they create a physical barrier that blocks visual access through windows, making it impossible to see through them regardless of other spatial relationships.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

While not explicitly listed in the rules, the spatial principle is straightforward: closed shutters (Evidence 5) physically prevent visibility through windows. This directly contradicts Testimony 3 where Andistan'dhin claims to have seen Ahlbi "through the window."

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial reasoning is simple: shutters that are closed (as stated in Evidence 5) block the line of sight through windows. Since Andistan'dhin's chamber can only see the treasure room through windows in the hallway (as per Evidence 6), and these windows had their shutters closed, it would be impossible for Andistan'dhin to see Ahlbi in the treasure room as claimed in Testimony 3.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Looking at Rimes's testimonies, he claims:
- Testimony 0: At 10:10 AM, he was in the staff room
- Testimony 1: He heard a loud noise from the pool room and went to the door to look in
- Testimony 2: He couldn't see the captain or the orca, but saw coins scattered around

However, the Photo of the Body (Evidence 2) shows the crime scene in the orca pool room. Given that the pool is 65 feet deep (Evidence 3) and the body was found in the pool, this creates a spatial contradiction with Rimes's claim that he "couldn't see the captain or the orca."

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial reasoning rules are:
- If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY
- If XXX is in a different room from YYY then XXX cannot see YYY
- If XXX is facing YYY then XXX can see YYY

**Step 3:** Apply the spatial reasoning rules

According to Testimony 1, Rimes went to the door to look into the pool room, which means he was at the doorway looking into the same room as the orca pool. The Photo of the Body (Evidence 2) shows the body was in the orca pool. Since Rimes was looking into the pool room from the doorway, he was in the same room as the orca and the body. According to the rule "If XXX is in the same room as YYY then XXX can potentially see YYY," Rimes should have been able to see both the captain (body) and the orca, contradicting his testimony that he "couldn't see the captain or the orca."

**Step 4:** The contradiction is clearly revealed by applying the spatial reasoning rule about visibility within the same room.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Shih-na's testimony states: "Although I was in the next room, I was unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu." This implies she was physically in the adjacent room but could not see the Yatagarasu.

However, the evidence shows that the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall (Evidence 29: "Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall and into the next room over"). This creates a spatial contradiction - if Shih-na was in the next room over, she would have been in the exact location where the Yatagarasu emerged from the revolving fireplace wall.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The relevant spatial reasoning rule is: "If XXX is touches YYY then YYY is touches XXX" - this implies that if Shih-na was in the next room (touching the space where the Yatagarasu emerged), then the Yatagarasu would have been touching the same space as Shih-na when exiting the revolving fireplace wall.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

The rule "If XXX is touches YYY then YYY is touches XXX" applies here. When the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall into the next room, they would have been in direct spatial contact with that room. Since Shih-na claims to have been in that same next room, she would have been in direct spatial contact with the Yatagarasu at the moment of emergence, making it impossible for her to not catch a glimpse of them.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction is revealed through basic spatial reasoning: the revolving fireplace wall leads directly into the next room, and if Shih-na was present in that room, she would have been in the immediate vicinity of the Yatagarasu's escape route, making her claim of not seeing the Yatagarasu highly implausible.

**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Lang's testimony claims the president's body was hidden "inside that monster costume over there" (Testimony 3), and that there's dirt inside the costume because it got there when the body was hidden inside (Testimony 4).

However, the Crime Scene Notes (Evidence 5) state: "The body lying face-up on top of the dirt, and yet there's no dirt on the front of the body." This creates a spatial contradiction - if the body was hidden inside the monster costume and got dirt inside it, then the body itself should have dirt on it, particularly on the front since it was lying face-up.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules from the list above apply to this contradiction

The spatial reasoning rules provided are primarily about relative positioning (inside, in front of, behind, to the left of, etc.) and visibility (blocks the view, cannot be seen). These rules don't directly address the contradiction between dirt being inside a costume versus no dirt being on the body itself.

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

No applicable spatial reasoning rules from the provided list directly address this contradiction about dirt presence/absence.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction is revealed through basic spatial reasoning: if a body is placed inside a costume that contains dirt, the body would necessarily come into contact with that dirt. The evidence shows the body had no dirt on its front side, which contradicts the claim that the body was hidden inside a dirty costume.

These rules don't directly address the contradiction between Simeon's claim of seeing a body double on the roof and the security footage showing only the real President Wang.

**Step 3:** Since none of the listed rules directly apply, we proceed to Step 4.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction.

The contradiction lies in the spatial presence/absence of individuals. Simeon claims to have seen a body double on the roof (Testimony 1 and 3), but the security camera footage (Evidence 10) shows only President Wang and Judge Gavèlle on the roof, with no indication of a body double. This creates a direct spatial contradiction - Simeon claims to have seen someone (the body double) who is not visible in the security footage that covers the same location.
